Research Report

2026年 11月 6日

1/7 
2014年 GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ 
～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ GPIFが基本ポートフォリオを変更し、国内債券の割合を引き上げるのではないかとの観測が
市場で浮き沈みしている。本稿では、現在の基本ポートフォリオの起点となった 2014年の運
用改革の成立過程を分析し、変更する場合にどのようなことが起き得るのか考察する。 
○ 2014年改革では、基本ポートフォリオに占める国内債券を60％から 35％へ大幅に引き下げ、
国内外の株式を約 2倍に増やした。アベノミクスの象徴の一つとして GPIF改革は語られる
ことが多いが、改革につながる議論は民主党政権期から始まっていた。 
○ 当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事録によれば、株式をどこまで増やすかより、国内債券をどこま
で減らすかが議論の中心だった。また、当時決定した国内債券 35％という配分は、期待収益
率やリスクだけで機械的に決まったものではなく、移行コストや市場への影響などを考慮し
て決定された。市場への影響を抑えるため、正式決定前から乖離許容幅を使って国内債券を
減らしていたのは、今後の動向を見極めるうえで興味深い。当面、四半期の運用実績に注目
が集まるだろう。 
○ 足元では長期金利が上昇し、低金利が懸念点だった 2014年当時とは逆の環境にある。当時の
運用委員会では、金利が上がれば国内債券の期待収益率が高まり、最適化をやり直すことで
国債の割合が上昇し得るとの説明がなされていた。当時の考え方を踏まえれば、現在の基本
ポートフォリオが「金利のある世界」でも最適なのか再考の余地があろう。

### Page 2

2/7 
1. はじめに 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）を巡り、基本ポートフォリオの見直し観測が市場で
浮き沈みしている。きっかけは片山さつき財務・金融担当相が2026年7月、GPIFをはじめとする
年金基金について、日本の金融資産への投資をさらに増やす方向を後押ししたいとの考えを示し
たことだ。長期金利が上昇するなかでの発言で、日本の金融資産とは特に国内債券だと解されて
いる。こうしたなか、GPIF側では同年 8月の経営委員会の議事次第に「基本ポートフォリオ検証
等PTにおける議論について」が報告事項に入り、経営委員会への関心が高まった。 
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは 5年に 1度、公的年金の財政検証を踏まえて見直す。現在の基本
ポートフォリオは2025年度が開始年だ。制度上、毎年度検証し、必要があれば 5年間の中期目標
期間中でも見直しを検討することになっているものの、GPIF発足後、中期目標期間の途中で基本
ポートフォリオを変更したのは 2013年 6月と 2014年 10月の 2回にとどまる。 
2014年10月の見直しでは、国内債券比率を 60％から 35％へ引き下げ、国内外の株式をそれぞ
れ12％から25％へ引き上げる大幅な変更が行われた。株式比率の上昇によって損失が大きくなる
リスクを懸念する向きはあったが、運用損失だけでなく、年金財政上、目標とする利回りを達成
できないこともリスクと捉える考え方を明確にした。2014年改革の本質は公的年金運用における
「安全」という概念を変えたことだと筆者はみている。 
では、仮にいま国内債券を再び増やすとなれば、改革の逆行を意味するのか。この問いを考え
るため、年金財政からの議論と成長戦略からの要請という二つの流れを民主党政権期からたど
り、2014年改革がどのように実現したのかを振り返る。

2. 民主党政権期――国債中心の「安全運用」への疑問 
2014年改革は第2次安倍晋三政権の経済政策「アベノミクス」の象徴の一つとして語られるこ
とが多い。しかし、その成立過程をさかのぼると、改革につながる議論は第 2次安倍政権の発足
前からあった。民主党政権期の議論では、公的年金の運用における「安全」を巡る議論が次第に
成熟していったことが確認できる。 
2006年に発足したGPIFの基本ポートフォリオは、当初国内債券が 67％を占めていた（資料 1）。
このうち、大半を占める国債は満期に額面で償還される。国債偏重の狙いは、厚生労働省の検討
会が 2010年12月にまとめた報告書にも表れている。公的年金運用の考え方として、保険料は
「投資を目的として徴収されたもの」ではないとして、「安全運用が基本」とした（注 1）。検討会
では、全額を国債で運用すべきだとの意見もあった。

### Page 3

3/7 
資料 1 GPIFの基本ポートフォリオの変遷

（注）2014年 10月以降は短期資産を基本ポートフォリオに含めない。 
（出所）GPIFの公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

もっとも、国債を多く持つことが本当に安全なのかという疑問はすでにこの検討会で示されて
いた。運用上のリスクを単なる損失だけで捉えず、年金財政上予定された収益を下回り、将来の
保険料率の引き上げや給付の引き下げにつながる可能性まで考慮する必要があるとしている。国
債を多く持てば安全だという単純な整理には収まらない議論が存在していたことが確認できる。 
この検討会に続く社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会では、国債への集中はリスクになると
いう考えが強く示されるようになる。2012年 2月に同委員会の議論を整理した資料（注2）では
国債のみの運用は一つの資産への集中であり、分散投資ができていないという意味でリスクが非
常に大きい、という指摘が確認できる。デフレと低金利がこの先も続くとは考えにくく、物価や
賃金が上昇すれば、給付する年金額は増える一方で債券価格は下落し、損失を被る可能性が高い
というアベノミクス下での改革を先取りするような意見も出た。国債のみの運用で、将来にわた
り物価や賃金の上昇率を上回る利回りを確保できるのか疑問が残る、との意見もあった。 
民主党政権後期の議論は、短期の運用損失を過度に重視するのではなく、国債偏重によって年
金財政上、必要な利回りを確保できないリスクを考慮する方向へと発展していった。

3. 産業競争力会議――成長戦略からの見直し要請 
GPIF改革に至る過程では、日本経済の成長戦略の文脈で国債偏重の見直しを求める意見があっ
た。これは安倍政権時だけでなく民主党政権期にもあった。国内債券を減らすべきだという方向
は、年金財政からの議論と重なる。ただ、見直しを求める理由は両政権期とも公的年金の巨額の
資金を経済成長に生かすという発想から来ている。 
安倍政権でGPIF改革の舞台となったのは、政権発足直後の2013年 1月に始まった産業競争力会
議だ。「公的・準公的資金の有効活用」が初回から議論にのぼり、安全資産に偏った年金などの運
用を見直し、成長分野への資金供給につなげる考え方が示された。同会議が進むなかでは、民間

### Page 4

4/7 
議員から「アクティブ運用をし、そこに中長期でもの言う株主ということで企業が新陳代謝も進
めるようなツールとしていく」といった発言が飛び出している（注 3）。 GPIFの資金を企業改革や
経済成長に生かそうとする考え方であり、「専ら被保険者の利益」のために運用するという年金積
立金の原則との緊張をはらんでいた。

4. 有識者会議――成長戦略と被保険者利益の両立 
成長戦略のなかで出たこうした要請を、公的年金の運用原則とどのように両立させたのだろう
か。この問題に対応したのが「公的・準公的資金の運用・リスク管理等の高度化等に関する有識
者会議」だった。この会議は政府の日本再興戦略を受けて経済再生担当相の下に設置され、GPIF
改革の理論的柱を打ち立てた。 
公的年金の運用に国民が何を最も期待しているのか明確に定義した点がこの会議のポイントだ
ろう。2013年 11月の報告書は、「現役世代の保険料負担を維持しつつ、将来の年金給付に支障が
生じないよう、年金財政の長期的な健全性の確保に資すること」こそが国民の最も期待している
ことだとした（注4）。目標とする運用利回りが達成できなければ、保険料の引き上げか将来の年
金給付水準の引き下げが迫られる可能性が生じる。 
この定義に則り、デフレから脱却して適度なインフレ環境へ移るというアベノミクスの目指し
た環境でのポートフォリオのあり方を検討した。安全資産とされる国債を多く持てば安全だと考
えるのではなく、年金財政に必要な収益を確保できるかという観点から基本ポートフォリオを考
える方向が明確に示された。 
収益率の向上という文脈のなかで、産業競争力会議で出たようなGPIFの資金を企業改革に生か
すというアイデアは運用改革に取り込まれていく。投資先企業との対話や議決権行使を通じて企
業価値の向上を促し、その成果を運用収益として享受するという考え方が報告書で示された。成
長戦略が期待した企業改革を、被保険者の利益につながる運用につなげたのだ。 
社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会もこれに呼応する形で、デフレから脱却して適度なイン
フレ環境へ移る状況では、あらかじめ「国内債券中心」と示す必要はなく、GPIFが運用目的・目
標に沿ったポートフォリオを検討すべきだとした。年金積立金の運用目標は、年金給付費が長期
的には名目賃金上昇率に連動することから、賃金上昇率をどの程度上回るかで設定される。同専
門委員会は、財政検証に向けて検討した複数の経済前提を踏まえ、GPIFが確保すべき運用利回り
として、名目賃金上昇率を 1.7％上回る水準を示した（注5）。

5. GPIF運用委員会――国内債券 35％の決定過程 
ここから2014年 10月の基本ポートフォリオ変更に至る議論を、当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事
録からたどる。2014年改革は、株式を増やした改革とみられがちだ。しかし、運用委員会の議論
の中心になったのは株式の比率ではなく、国内債券の扱いだった。後年公表された議事録を読む
と、国内債券中心では目標の利回りが達成できないどころか、金利が上昇した場合には多額の損
失を被るといった懸念が、当時の事務局や理事長から示されている。当時の長期金利は 0.5％前後

### Page 5

5/7 
と、歴史的な低水準にあった（資料 2）。事務局の試算も、国内債券の比率を 45％以下に減らさな
ければ目標の利回りに届かない、という形で示された。

資料 2 日本国債 10年物利回り

（注）10年物国債利回りの月末値。2026年 11月は 6日の値。 
（出所）Bloombergより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

GPIF内部の議論では株式をどこまで増やすかではなく、国内債券をどこまで減らすかが議論の
焦点だったことが分かる。運用委員会の委員長は最終段階で、「国債を減らしたというのが一番の
主で、その結果として、こういうポートフォリオが書けた」と振り返っているのが象徴的だろう
（注 6）。 
基本ポートフォリオの決定に至る議論で興味深いのは、GPIFの売買が国内市場に与える影響を
相当程度考慮に入れていた点だ。事務局から示された基本ポートフォリオ候補は当初、五つあっ
た。このなかには、現在のポートフォリオである 4資産を 25％ずつとする案も含まれていた。こ
の案は、現状からの移行を考えると「現実感がゼロ」とされ、早い段階で検証の対象から外れた
（注 7）。事務局は候補を絞り込む際に足元の資産構成からの距離を物差しに加え、最終案の選定
理由にも「足下の資産構成と相対的に近く、移行に係るコストが小さい」ことを挙げている（注
8）。最終段階では、1％刻みで確認すると、経済中位ケースでは国内債券 34％などのより効率的な
配分も見つかった。それでも、国内債券をさらに減らして国内株式を増やせば移行の影響が大き
くなるとして、35％が維持された。外国市場に比べ、国内市場ではGPIFの売買の影響が大きいと
いう判断が働いていた。 
国内債券の売却は、正式な決定を待たずに始まっていた。運用委員会は2014年4月の時点で、
現行の基本ポートフォリオが認める範囲の中で国内債券を減らす方針を了承している。委員長は

### Page 6

6/7 
その目的を、金利上昇に備えて「債券の評価損を抑える」ことだと確認した（注9）。8月には、
その範囲を超えて国内債券を売り、ほかの資産に振り向ける方針も了承された。事務局は、積立
金規模が巨額であるため、市場インパクトやフロントランニングを誘発せずに速やかに資産配分
を変更することは現実的ではないと説明し、基本ポートフォリオの正式決定前から資産配分の変
更を検討する考えを示した（注 10）。正式決定に先立つ 8月末には、国内債券の比率はすでに
50％程度まで下がっていた。 
GPIFの現在の中期目標でも、市場の価格形成や民間の投資行動等を歪めないことが求められて
いる。今後、基本ポートフォリオを変更する場合にも、市場への影響を抑えるため、正式な変更
に先立って資産配分を調整することが考えられる。今回も運用の動向を見極めるうえで、四半期
の運用実績が注目される。

6. おわりに 
2014年にGPIFが中期目標期間の途中で基本ポートフォリオを変更した背景には、先行きの運用
環境の変化を見通した判断があった。GPIFは当時、日本経済が「長年続いたデフレからの転換と
いう大きな運用環境の変化の節目」にあるとし、「長期的な経済環境の変化に速やかに対応する観
点から、来年度を待たず」に変更に踏み切った（注 11）。実際には低金利がその後も長く続いた
が、足元では当時懸念されていた金利上昇が現実のものとなっている。 
長期金利は2026年 9月に 3％台に達し、約 30年ぶりの水準を付けた。奇しくも3％という数字
は、2014年のGPIF運用委員会でもたびたび登場していた。当時の理事長は、国内債券 60％という
従来の基本ポートフォリオについて、「国内債券の利回りが 3％というものを大前提としてつくっ
ている」と述べていた（注 12）。  
興味深いのは、2014年の運用委員会で、金利が上昇すれば国内債券の将来の期待収益率が高ま
り、改めて資産配分の最適化を行うことで「自然に国債の割合がまたもとに戻るというか、上が
っていく」とGPIFの事務局側が説明していたことだ（注 13）。 2020年の基本ポートフォリオ変更
では、国内金利の低下を踏まえ、国内債券を減らして外国債券を増やした。今後は、長期の収益
確保を担う株式の比率を維持しつつ、為替リスクなどを考慮して外国債券の一部を国内債券に振
り向けることも選択肢として考えられる。2014年に国内債券を減らしたのと同じ考え方が、今度
はその割合を引き上げる判断につながり得るのではないだろうか。

1

堅調なAI 需要で大幅増益が視野 
投資増につながるかが焦点に

○ 製造業の業績見通しは大幅に改善している。輸出向けを中心とするAI関連財の需要拡大が背景。
2026年度は好業績が期待され、二桁増益が視野に入る 
○ AI関連財の増産を背景に、国内製造業の設備不足感は2025年後半以降強まっている。足元では関
連業種の機械受注が増加しており、AI需要拡大が国内設備投資に波及する兆しがみられる 
○ 設備投資は緩やかな拡大が息長く続く見通し。ただし、米国でデータセンター建設規制が実施さ
れれば、輸出・業績・設備投資の好循環が逆回転に転じるリスクも

１．企業景況感は AI 関連の製造業を中心に改善 
AIの学習・利用に必要なデータセンター投資や半導体需要が世界的に堅調であることを背景に、日
本の製造業の景況感は幅広く改善している。日銀短観（2026年9月調査）では、大企業・製造業の業況
判断DIが6月調査から改善した（図表1）。業種別では、半導体や電子部品などを含む電気機械や、半
導体製造装置を含む生産用機械の改善が際立つ。そのほか、窯業・土石製品、非鉄金属、半導体材料・
素材を扱う化学などでも改善がみられた。ロイター短観（10月調査）でも、「半導体関連市場の需要
が強い」との声が散見され、AI関連需要の堅調さがうかがえる。 
AI関連財の輸出が拡大し、足元で企業の業績見通しは大きく改善している。企業業績の改善を国内
の需要拡大につなげるうえで、次の焦点となるのが設備投資である。企業業績の改善に比べて、投資
図表 1 業況判断 DI 図表 2 日本の AI 関連財輸出額

2

などの内需への恩恵はまだ明確ではなく、今後設備投資の増加として発現する可能性が期待される。 
２．AI 関連財輸出は好調。2026 年度の製造業は二桁増益が視野に 
世界的なAIサービスの普及に伴い、データセンターや関連機器への投資が拡大している。大規模な
データセンターを保有・運営し、クラウドサービスやAI向けの計算基盤を提供するIT企業、とりわけ
ハイパースケーラーと呼ばれる大規模事業者を中心に、高水準の設備投資を継続する見通しが示され
ていることを踏まえると、AI関連投資の高い伸びは当面持続する見込みである。こうしたなか、モデ
ルの演算を担う処理装置のほか、大量のデータを蓄える記憶装置、サーバー同士をつなぐ通信ケーブ
ル、電力を安定供給する電源装置などといった財において投資需要が高まっている。日本企業が供給
する財についても、電子部品・機器への需要が増加しているほか、AI向け半導体の増産投資は日本の
半導体製造・検査装置や材料への需要を押し上げている。 
図表2は、輸出全体から、WTOの定義に従ってAI関連財を抽出したものだ。AI関連財輸出は2025年終
盤以降加速しており、8月時点では前年比＋29.1％と高い伸びになった。主因は半導体・電子部品で、
旺盛な需要のもとで半導体価格が大幅に上昇し、輸出金額を押し上げている。また、半導体製造・検
査機器や、データセンターに組み込まれる機械・部材、半導体の製造に用いられる原材料・素材など、
幅広いAI関連財が輸出増に寄与している。 
輸出額の高い伸びを受け、製造業では業績見通しが改善している。日銀短観（9月調査）の2026年度
経常利益計画（全規模・製造業）は、6月調査時点から大幅に上方修正され、前年度比＋11.6％の増益
が見込まれる（図表3）。特に電気機械では、上期の堅調な輸出実績を反映して、経常利益計画が大き
く上方修正された。このほか、業況判断DIの改善が目立った業種でも同様の傾向がみられた。堅調な
AI関連需要が下期も続けば、製造業では大幅増益が期待される。

３．AI 関連業種の投資需要は増勢加速の兆しも 
今後の実質GDPへの影響を見通すうえでの焦点は、企業収益の拡大が設備投資を中心とした内需の押
し上げにどの程度繋がるかだ。この点、AI需要の追い風を受け好業績が見込まれることを踏まえれば、
設備投資の伸びはやや物足りなさが否めない。日銀短観（9月調査）によると、製造業の2026年度設備
投資計画は直近5年間では最も低い伸びに留まっている（図表4）。建材や生産設備の価格上昇、金利
上昇に伴う資金調達コストの増加が投資負担となり、好業績のもとでも計画の積み増しを抑えている
可能性がある。 
もっとも、設備投資需要は計画対比で伸び余地がある。AI関連財を中心に増産が進むなか、能力増
強投資の必要性が高まっているためだ。AI関連財の生産は2025年秋以降、はっきりとした増加傾向が
続いている（図表5）。生産・営業用設備判断DI（全規模・製造業）も2025年後半から低下しており、
製造業の設備不足感は強まっている（図表6）。 
実際、AI需要の拡大が今後の設備投資を押し上げる兆しもみられる。図表7は、設備投資の先行指標
とされる機械受注（国内需要向け）を需要者別に分解し、価格変動の影響を取り除いたものだ。これ
をみると2025年夏場以降、情報通信機械、電気機械、はん用・生産用機械といった業種からの受注が
急増していることがわかる。結果として、半導体製造装置をはじめとするAI・データセンター関連品
目では受注残高が積み上がっている（図表8）。 
ただし、機械受注が設備投資として顕在化するまでにはタイムラグを伴うとみられる。そもそも、
受注残高が積み上がっている背景には、旺盛な需要だけでなく、供給面の制約による影響があると考
えられる1。このため、設備投資の増加ペースは緩やかなものにとどまる公算が大きいとみているが、
その分、今後は息の長い投資拡大局面が続くだろう。

図表 5 鉱工業生産 図表6 生産・営業用設備判断DI

４．今後は政府の投資支援策も民間投資を後押し。米国の規制強化リスクには要注意 
以上のように、先行きの設備投資は、設備不足感の強まるAI関連産業の投資需要に支えられ、緩や
かに増勢を強めると見込まれる。 
また今後は、政府の政策支援が、投資コストの負担軽減を通じて、設備投資を後押しするとみられ
る。7月末に始動した政府の「大胆な投資促進税制」では、一定の要件を満たす国内投資について、即
時償却や税額控除の適用を認める減税措置が設けられた。さらに、2027年度予算の編成では、特別予
算枠として『「強く豊かな日本」投資枠』を設け、民間投資を促す支援策が検討されている。具体的
な支援策が整えば、複数年度にわたる投資計画の実行を支えよう。 
なお、11月3日に投開票された米国中間選挙の結果を受け、米国におけるデータセンター投資が下振
れるリスクにも触れておきたい。同選挙では、データセンター建設計画の多いテキサス州やジョージ
ア州の州知事選などにおいて、建設の一時停止を掲げる民主党候補が勝利した。米国では、データセ
ンター建設規制に関して州政府や地方自治体が大きな権限を持つ。 
実際、市場ではAI関連企業の業績・投資下振れが意識され、グローバルにハイテク銘柄の株価が急
落した。仮に今後、米国でデータセンター建設が規制されれば、日本でも半導体・電子部品や製造装
置などの輸出が減少し、企業業績が下押しされる可能性がある。その際、AI関連財が減産に転じて設
備不足感が弱まれば、日本国内における能力増強投資の需要も後退しかねない。新知事の任期開始お
よび新議会の招集は2027年1月だが、当面の米国政治動静については注意深くみていく必要があろう。

1 なお、供給制約に起因して、資本財の輸入浸透度（国内供給に占める輸入品の割合）が一層上昇する可能性には留意が必要で
ある。今後、国内の供給余力が限られるなかで投資需要が一層輸入品に向かえば、国産資本財への需要が減退し、投資需要増に
よる GDP の押し上げ効果が限られるリスクがある。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 11 月 16 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 黃仁勳請辭衝擊輝達，AI 需求韌性支撐科技股反彈：輝達執行長黃仁勳因涉及國安疑慮的相
關爭議，11/12 正式辭任執行長及董事職務，由財務長 Colette Kress 暫代，輝達當日股價盤
中一度重挫近 12%，惟隨後跌幅收斂收跌 4.3%。領導層更迭引發市場對公司策略及 AI 產業
發展的疑慮，但 CoreWeave 第三季營收年增 168%，因受惠 AI 算力需求強勁，此一利多顯
示 AI 需求尚未顯著轉弱，惟輝達管理團隊穩定性、後續財報及政策調查進展，仍是影響科技

股評價的重要變數。
◼ 美國 10 月通膨高於預期，Fed 升息預期推升美債殖利率：美國 11/10 公布 10 月 CPI 月增
0.35%，高於預期的 0.3%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，亦高於預期的 0.2%，顯示通膨壓力仍具黏
性。因 2025 年政府停擺造成資料缺漏，本次未公布年增率。Fed 理事 Waller 表示，若核心
通膨維持 0.3%左右，12 月應考慮升息 2 碼，市場因此提高對後續緊縮的預期。
◼ 伊朗上週末再度對美軍設施進行攻擊，惟美方尚無反擊行動：美國國務卿魯比歐 11/13 表示，
美國對伊朗造成的經濟壓力已大到沒有必要採取軍事行動。不過，伊朗革命衛隊上週末以無人
機攻擊美軍在巴林的雷達站，但美軍尚無進一步反擊，留意之後美方是否仍以封鎖取代攻擊。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短期審慎、中期仍正向，AI 供應鏈基本面持續具支撐：黃仁勳辭任使輝達面臨管理層
接班、企業治理及政策調查等不確定性，加上 Fed 升息預期及 AI 資料中心監管疑慮，市場
短線波動可能延續。然而，該事件並未直接改變 AI 算力需求，且上週 CoreWeave Q3 財報

營收年增 168%、台積電 10 月營收年增 59.7%，均顯示 AI 基礎建設投資仍具動能。整體對
科技股維持短期審慎、但中長期基本面仍在，台灣及日本等具技術競爭優勢、獲利與現金流
穩健的半導體及 AI 供應鏈亦值得留意。惟市場焦點已由資本支出規模轉向投資報酬與現金
流，後續仍須關注輝達財報及管理團隊能否維持客戶信心。
◼ Fed 升息預期壓抑債市，中天期美債及投資級債仍相對具吸引力： 美國 10 月 CPI 高於預
期，加上 Fed 官員偏鷹表態，使債券市場持續承受政策利率上修壓力。美國 10 年期公債殖
利率重回 5%以上，收益水準已具吸引力。相較之下，短天期債券雖然價格波動較小，但殖
利率較低，且未來仍面臨政策利率及再投資風險；超長天期債券則易受到財政赤字、發債供
給與期限溢價影響。中天期美債在收益與利率風險之間相對均衡，維持審慎正向看法。信用
債方面，投資級債收益條件仍具支撐，惟須留意高資本支出企業的融資與現金流風險。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 隨著法國距離下屆總統大選僅剩六個月，極左翼與政府間的政治對立升溫，然而
法國政府急需推動預算案以控制財政赤字與龐大債務。在內政危機加劇與工會
預告 11 月大罷工的背景下，市場對法國債務可持續性的擔憂升溫，將帶動法德
公債利差維持高檔，進而推升歐元區主權風險溢酬並拖累歐元表現。
 上週路透最新調查顯示，在 100 位受訪經濟學家中，高達 94%預期聯準會將於
10 月會議維持利率在 3.75%-4.00% 不變，惟全員一致預估 12 月將重啟升息。
儘管美國受 AI 投資熱潮帶動、經濟成長強勁，且美伊戰事推升油價突破百元
大關、加徵關稅使 PCE 通膨至少在 2028 年前難以回到 2% 目標，官員仍傾
向審慎行事。不過逾四成專家警告，聯準會恐落後通膨形勢，後續面臨至少再升
息兩次甚至擴大升息幅度的上行風險，預期美元大幅回檔的空間小，逢低承接買
盤依然強勁。
 路透最新調查顯示，受日圓疲軟與中東戰事推升能源及進口成本、加劇通膨壓力

影響，近七成受訪經濟學家預計日本央行將於 12月再度升息至 1.50%，延續 6
月與 9 月以來的較快緊縮步伐；同時，逾半數專家預估終端利率將上看 2.00%
或更高。不過，高達近四分之三受訪者警告，若高市早苗內閣提名的鴿派委員再
次投下反對票，恐壓抑日圓表現。在歐美央行年底前亦可能升息的背景下，日本
央行維持較快的緊縮節奏，有助於防止美日、歐日利差進一步擴大，避免日圓在
年底遭遇新一輪劇烈拋售，為日圓提供實質的下檔保護。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.360，低點在 32.704，以 32.508 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，台幣跳空開低，跌破今
年 7 月的低點 32.528；隨著台股表現不佳，外資賣超台股並匯出，台幣續創低位，
最低來到 32.704周內低點；而後台股低檔反彈，外資由匯出轉為雙向進出，加以出
口商積極進場拋售美金，台幣由貶轉升至 32.500下方；臨近周末，市場擔憂颱風來
襲周五可能休市，增添連續假期變數，外資再度大幅賣超台股並匯出，令台幣又跌
回 32.600上方，終場接在央行調節之下收在 32.508。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。近期全球科技股因 AI 類股估值偏高及融資風險疑慮
而持續修正，市場風險偏好降溫，股市面臨修正，外資連續賣超台股並匯出，台幣
走勢持續面臨壓力。另一方面，美伊戰事和談進展的消息反覆，令國際油價節節攀
升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，引發通膨升溫的擔憂，支撐利率維持在高檔的預期，
美元指數也在 101.5 上方高位震盪，將不利台幣的表現。盤勢上來看，外資隨著股
市回檔修正表現偏向賣超台股並匯出，台幣偏弱，不過本周適逢月底，預計匯價逢
高仍持續有出口商拋售美金的需求，將限制台幣走貶的幅度。綜合以上，預估本週
台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/27/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣走升。周初中國公布 9月工業生產物價指數 5.5%，表現優於預期的
5.4%(前值 5.2%)，且第三季 GDP 表現 4.5%，也優於預期的 4.4%(前值 4.3%)，基本
面穩健支撐人民幣升破 6.6800 整數關口；不過周間美元指數持續於 101.5 上方高位
震盪，加以美伊戰事未見緩解，國際油價持續飆升，突破每桶 110 美元上方，人民
幣於 6.6800下方高檔震盪；臨近周末，人行中間價格連續走升，升破 6.7200整數關
口，週五最高來到 6.7180，顯示中國人行允許人民幣溫和升值的走勢，終場境外人
民幣升破 6.6700整數關口，收在 6.6690，週線升值 160點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6704，
週線升值 166 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價持續偏升，將持續帶
動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現；就國際市場來看，最
新利率期貨顯示，本周聯準會利率決議有 9成的機率維持利率在 3.75%~4.00%不變，
不過年底前仍有一碼的升息空間，加以地緣政治風險未見緩解，能源價格高檔震盪
推升通膨與利率預期，美元指數維持在 101.5 上方高檔震盪，將限制人民幣升值的
速度跟幅度；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價
維持在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6300-6.6900

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌。週初，USD/JPY 大致在 158 的整數關卡上下波動；
週間，AI相關概念股遭拋售，加之中東局勢緊張推升避險需求，10年期美債殖利率
跌破 5%，避險資金亦流入日圓，帶動 USD/JPY 下行最低來至 156.67；臨近週末，
市場避險需求持續加之日本 9 月通膨數據高於日本央行 2%目標，USD/JPY 一度下
跌至 156.06，後逢低買盤進場跌幅有所收斂，終場收在 156.86，週線下跌 113 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下： 一、美國 10 月 S&P製造業 PMI
為 56.0高於 9 月的 55.9且為 53個月高點，服務業則為 57.9低於 9月的 58.8，綜合
PMI為 57.6亦低於 9 月的 58.4，顯示美國商業活動雖略有放緩但仍處於擴張，經濟
基本面依然穩健將持續支撐美元；此外，中東局勢惡化使能源供應風險上升，布蘭
特原油價格突破 116 美元，為六個月以來高點，也將有利 USD/JPY上行。二、日本
9 月全國整體消費者物價指數（CPI）年增 2.1%，且 9 月核心 CPI年增 2.1%，為九
個月以來首次超過日本央行 2%的目標，強化市場對日本央行短期升息的預期，預計
為日圓帶來支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY匯價處於各天期均線之
下且接連跌破 158 及 157 關卡，後在 156 整數關卡暫時獲得支撐，顯示逢低仍有買
盤進場，將限縮下行幅度。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較
高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：155.00-158.50

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Oct/27/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD震盪下跌。週初，EUR/USD在 1.1200整數關卡窄幅波動；週間
受到油價及美債殖利率回落，外加法國社會局勢略見緩解影響，帶動匯價反彈至
1.1260遇到賣壓後回落，後續大致在 1.1190至 1.1250 間波動；臨近週末，歐元區 10
月份 PMI表現低迷，且市場對於法國政局和財政赤字擔憂持續，拖累 EUR/USD轉
若，匯價一度下行來至 1.1183，終場收在 1.1202，週線下跌 37點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏空，主要原因如下：一、上週公佈歐元區 10月 PMI
顯示德、法表現分化，法國製造業及服務業均表現疲弱低於 50榮枯線，拖累歐元區
10 月綜合 PMI 由 9 月 53.1 下滑至 51.9，為四個月以來新低，經濟基本面承壓將不
利 EUR/USD上行；此外，法國社會及政經局勢不穩導致法債殖利率突破 5%，市場
對其財政及政治危機擔憂持續，也將不利歐元表現。二、美國上週初請失業金人數
降至 19.4萬人，為三個月以來新低，顯示勞動力市場依然穩健，且 10月 S&P 製造
業 PMI為 56.0續創 53個月新高，服務業及綜合 PMI維持穩健，顯示美國經濟仍具
韌性，預期將限縮 EUR/USD上行空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD
各天期均線維持空頭排列，且匯價持續承壓於月線之下，顯示短線空方趨勢仍未改
變，在能有效突破上方月線前仍以震盪偏空看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週
呈現震盪偏空的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1120-1.1270

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.19-115.10.22 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
  公債－10年期- A15109 2.000%~2.002% 
      － 5年期- A15108     1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，儘管市場預期強烈颱風「薔琵」來襲，週五金融市場可能
因颱風假而暫停交易，銀行間多預留帳上資金以應不時之需，但市況仍屬充
裕，即便有新增調度需求，也很容易滿足，令短率朝區間低位移動。 
週四隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.65%~1.68%，利率低位較上週下跌約 1 個基本點。

二、 本週市場交投冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109 週四於等殖系統成交在 2.002%，
5 年期指標券 A15108則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.88%。 
週二 10 年期公債標售得標利率 2.001%，落於市場預期區間下緣，並創 2008
年以來新高，顯示市場持續反映經濟成長與通膨預期。 
考量通膨前景及國際債市走勢仍具不確定性，市場觀望氣氛濃厚；惟第四季公
債供給相對有限，可望抑制殖利率大幅彈升。預期短線台債殖利率維持區間整
理，5 年券及 10 年券交易區間分別估於 1.82%~1.84%及 1.98%~2.01%。

Key Takeaways
We continue to see margin improvement, with October NPM at 23.4%, above
20.1% in 3Q26.
Margin improvement likely driven by combination of better pricing, improving
mix, and higher utilization for ABF and BT substrates.
Stay OW on both Unimicron and NYPCB – but on a 12-month view, we prefer
Unimicron (12.4x CY27e P/E) over NYPCB (13.0 CY27e P/E).

4
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Unimicron (3037.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate
value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.2%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 14%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF substrate demand from PC and server customers

n Capex cuts; halt to its capacity expansion plan
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.,
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition
n Yield issues or production hiccups when ramping new capacity
Nan Ya PCB (8046.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate

value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.3%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 17%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF and BT demand
n Faster-than-expected pickup in AI and 5G demand
n Stronger-than-expected ASP hike
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.

CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall, creating headwinds for ABF substrate demand and pricing
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

公司拜訪快報

融程電(3416)：國防軍工引領成長 
建議：維持買進

一、融程電為強固型電腦廠商，
近年國防軍工營收大幅提升 
 融程電成立於 1996 年，公司前三大股東為研華、醫揚、廣積。融程電為強固型
平板電腦和手持式裝置專業廠商，產品廣泛應用於航太、船舶、軍警、交通運輸、
車廠維修檢測、醫管、石化、和製程控制等利基型產業；歐洲為融程電最大市場，
賓士、保時捷、Bosch 等皆為融程電客戶，美洲市場方面，智慧倉儲解決方案廠
商 Honeywell、堆高機品牌 CROWN 以及智慧醫療 Medtronic 等亦是融程電客
戶。近年國防軍工營收大幅提升，自 2024 年的高個位數佔比提升至 2025 年的
近兩成水準，2026 年已突破兩成水準；截至 3Q26 Edge AI 佔比約 10%。

二、3Q26 毛利率低於預期，係
因出貨較多倉儲客戶產品 
 3Q26 營收 14.13 億元，截至 3Q26 產品組合：車檢 21%、倉儲物流 23%、醫
療保健 12%、其他 44%(其中超過 25%為國防軍工)；獲利方面，毛利率 36.36%，
低於預期，係因 3Q26 出貨較多低毛利率 Honeywell 倉儲物流產品，營益率
19.05%，單季稅後 EPS 2.63 元。

三、2027 年仍由國防軍工驅動主
要成長 
 3Q26 毛利率受 Honeywell 倉儲物流產品出貨較多影響，Honeywell 2026 全年
訂單金額預估 2~3 億元，2027 年 Honeywell 目標回到往年全年 6 億營收水準，
目前仍在與客戶洽談新訂單；4Q26 軍工航太及車用等出貨比重提升，預期毛利
率較 3Q26 回升；整體預期 2026 全年營收+30%YOY，其中出貨量成長 10%，
其餘 20%來自 ASP 提升(包含漲價與產品組合轉佳)。 
融程電目前 BB Ratio 1.1，訂單能見度約 6~8 個月，由於缺料狀況緩解，客戶
下單能見度恢復正常水準，預期 2027 年受惠軍工航太、無人載具及 Edge AI 需
求成長，產品組合持續優化將推升 ASP 及毛利率。產品組合方面，截至 3Q26
其他業務營收佔比 44%，2027 年佔比有望提升至 50%，主由國防軍工驅動，
2027 年國防軍工預期再成長 30~40%，甚至有望上看超過 50%成長，融程電具
全球領先海事船舶電腦技術，拓展至地面工作站及無人機整機，無人載具現有月

對 Interplex 的收購，10 月已完整貢獻營收，單月貢獻金額高達

的 Power 產品也持續提高出貨，研究部預期產品組合有望再度優
化，進一步推升獲利率表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 26Q4 營
收 349.1 億元，Q+25.3%，Y+76.5%，毛利率 33.84%，稅後淨利
56.7 億元，Q+32.0%，Y+106.8%，EPS 28.39 元；2026 年營收
1069.3 億元，Y+50.1%，毛利率 31.83%，稅後淨利 152.1 億元，
Y+69.0%，EPS 76.23 元。

2027 年 HPC 產品維持價量齊揚，光通訊產品 2028 年有望發酵 
隨著 Vera Rubin 進入放量，Rack Busbar 升級至 5000A，Power 
Whip 規格從 60A 升級至 100A 且用量增加，1.6T AEC 也將逐步
開始貢獻營收，另外 Interplex 的加入所帶來的產品優化和客戶關
係的綁定，研究部認為貿聯 HPC 事業仍將迎來顯著的價量齊揚增
長。光傳輸領域上貿聯也持續耕耘，除了新富生 MPO 相關產品，
OCP 上與 ams Osram 的合作也意味著公司已著手布局下一代
Scale up 的通訊方案，研究部看好 wide-and-slow 架構在未來的導
入機會以及市場，預計 2028 年後的發酵有望帶來營運成長動能。
綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2027 年營收 1608.0 億元，Y+50.4%，

結論 
高毛利產品合計來到 59%，此外營運槓桿帶來的效益使本業獲利率創歷
史新高。Interplex 已完整併表，單月貢獻金額來到 6700 萬美金，除了
擴大產品線外，合併後同樣有助於加深既有客戶的合作關係。展望 2027
年，HPC 事業將迎來產品價量齊揚，尤其以高毛利的 Power 產品最為明
顯，根據客戶規劃，1.6T AEC 也將逐步起量，因此可預期 HPC 占比將
會再進一步提升，利於整體營運表現。綜合以上，研究部預估貿聯 2026
年 EPS 76.23 元，2027 年 EPS 131.41 元，目前 PE 為 11.6x，參考歷
史 PE 區間為 10-35x，考量貿聯獲利將維持高成長期，唯獨近期市場擔
憂資料中心建置速度恐受高利率環境而放緩，存在一定 AI 伺服器延宕部
屬之風險，因此研究部下修 PER 至 22x，目標價 2900 元，維持買進。

Document 258: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

### Page 1

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Earnings Review Company Analysis | 이차전지/화학 | 2026. 11. 4  
일회성 보상금 포함된 실적, ‘30년 ESS 수요 대비 배 이상 높은 셀 3사 Capa 우려

AMPC 4,169억원 포함 영업이익 컨센서스 대폭 상회 
동사는 8일 매출액 9조 6,434억원(YoY 59%, QoQ 27%), AMPC 포함한 영업이익 
7,560억원(OPM 7.8%, YoY 26%, QoQ 567%)로 시장 Consensus(공정공시한 8일 
기준 3,264억원)을 대폭 상회하는 어닝 서프라이즈 발표. 회사는 ESS 출하 급증과 고
전압 Mid-Ni EV 파우치 확대, 견조한 EV향 2170-46시리즈 공급을 물량 중심을 성
장하였고, 폴란드 가동률 개선, 수익성이 견조한 원통형 물량 증가, 북미 ESS 생산성 
개선에 따른 고정비 부담 완화 및 고객사의 일회성 물량 보상금 영향으로 설명. 그러나 
일회성 물량 보상금의 구체적 금액, 반복 발생 여부는 제시하지 않아 불확실성 남겨. 
7,560억원의 영업이익에도 불구하고 영업외손실 9,872억원 발생으로 당기순손실 기
록. 이에는 소형 IT파우치 자산손상 반영되어, 가동률 개선 및 감가상각비 감소를 위해 
Capa를 조정하는 배터리 회사들의 대응 전략이 이번 실적에서 다시 확인

‘30년 ESS 수요 전망들 대비 국내사 Capa 배 이상 높아 
동사는 북미 ESS Capa 확대를 통한 탈중국 수요에 적극 대응하겠다는 전략. 파우치 
라인 추가 전환 및 신규 각형 라인 확보를 통해 북미 ESS Capa를 ‘26년말 50+에서 
‘27년 70+로 제시. 최근 타사에서 ‘26년 미국 ESS 수요를 80~100GWh, 혹은 
130+GWh로, ‘30년 두배 이상, 혹은 240+GWh로 수요 전망에 대해 답변한 바 있음. 
동사 전체 Capa 300GWh(‘25년 연말), SK On 179GWh, SDI 140GWh)임을 감안하
면 ‘30년 약 2배 수요를 가정하면 셀 3사 Capa의 32%에 달하는 수요, 만일 3배 수
요 가정하면 48.5% 수준. 북미 외 시장 가성비 중시 기조 및 중국 LFP 장악(‘25년 
97% M/S)으로 진입장벽 높음을 감안하면 미국 수요 중요. 이를 상쇄하기 위해 EV향 
배터리의 안정적인 공급 지속 필요하며, 이를 위해 동사 원통형은 TESLA의 비미국 시
장 판매량이, 파우치는 시장 자체 축소 지속 여부에 유의할 필요 
상대적으로 가장 견조한 출하량, 그러나 고평가 영역  
셀3사중 EV향 배터리 출하량 및 ESS 탑재에서 현재 가장 견조한 판매량을 보이고 있
으나, AMPC와 일회성 보상금이 영업손익의 상당부분을 견인한다는 점에서 수익성 취
약하다는 단점. 또한 ROE가 (-) 영역 임에도 PBR이 3.x내외로 거래되고 있어 고평가 
지적에서 자유롭기 어려워. 당분간 모회사인 LG화학의 PRS 발행에 따른 단기 오버행 
이슈도 관건. PBR 2.3x(‘25년대비 하락하는 ROE 감안, 20% 할인)적용 목표주가 
290,000원 제시, HOLD 유지. 주식 투자 매력 낮은 상황으로 판단 
 Analyst 정경희 
khchung@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 2

LS Securities Research 2 
EV향 3분기 data  
이차전지에서 EV향 비중은 여전히 가장 높다. 타입별로 보면 3분기 글로벌 EV향 이차전
지에서 각형의 M/S가 83%로 지속 증가하고 있으며, 동사가 생산하는 원통형과 파우치의 
경우 합산 M/S가 17%로 하락하였다. 현재 Wh 기준 동사 EV향 배터리에서 3분기 파우
치가 55%, 원통형이 45%로 파우치 비중이 10% 원통형을 상회한다. 
그림1 LGES 원통형, 파우치 타입 EV 향 분기별 탑재량 추이 
그림2

자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터

그림 2 EV 향 폼팩터 M/S: 각형 확장 vs 원통형/파우치 감소  그림 3 동사 EV 배터리 타입 비중

자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터  자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터

SNE data에 따르면 글로벌 원통형 EV향 수요의 대다수가 TESLA에서 발생하고 있으며, 
‘24~’25년 역성장후 ‘26년 판매량 회복을 나타내는 TESLA 호조로 전년대비 LGES의 EV
향 원통형 판매량은 3분기 증가했다. 반면 파우치의 경우 ‘25년 파우치 탑재 EV 판매 호조
를 나타낸 현대기아차그룹 영향으로 YoY 탑재량이 증가했으나, 올해는 북미 OEM인 GM, 
Ford향 판매가 IRA 일몰로 전년대비 부진함에 따라 3분기 전년대비 25% 감소한 것으로 
나타난다.

LS Securities Research 4 
그림 9 셀 3 사 분기별 EV 향 탑재량 글로벌 M/S 추이: M/S 감소중

자료: SNE 리서치, LS증권 리서치센터

밸류에이션, 수익성 대비 고평가 영역  
동사는 ‘24년 ROE -4.9%, ‘25년 -5.2%에 이어 ‘26년 -4.4%, ‘27년 -3.4%로 (-) 영역이 
지속되는 것으로 예상한다. ‘24년과 ‘25년 순이익은 (+)지만, 해외 합작회사가 많고 이들 이
익을 연결함에 따라 지배주주순이익은 적자를 기록한 것을 보인다. JV 규모가 축소되고 있
어 이러한 순이익과 지배주주 순이익상의 괴리는 좁혀지겠지만, ROE가 (-)영역 지속을 예
상함에 따라 현재 거래 PBR은 고평가 상황으로 볼 수 있다. 물론 이것은 향후 수익 성장
에 대한 시장 기대가 반영된 것으로 볼 수 있지만, 이차전지 6개사 평균 Consensus 대비 
실제 수익이 ‘23년부터 지속 하향되고 있다는 점, ESS대비 여전히 판매비중이 높은 EV에서 
출하량 정체가 일어나고 있다는 점, 미국 ESS에서 여전히 높은 중국 M/S를 감안하면 이러
한 기대 수준에 대해 다시 검토할 필요가 있다. 이에 더해, 모회사 기준에서는 필요한 과정
이지만 동사 주가 측면에서는 LG화학의 PRS 발행도 단기 오버행으로 당분간 주가에 부담
을 줄 수 있다.

그림 10 셀 3 사, 양∙음극재 3 사 추정치 변화율: ‘23 년부터 추정치 지속 하향 조정

자료: DataGuide, LS증권 리서치센터

미국 ESS가 모든 것을 해결할 수 있나? 
‘25년 글로벌 ESS 시장의 97%를 중국배터리사가 공급했다. LFP 각형이 표준화된 시장인 
것이다. 미국 시장만 중국에 대한 관세 허들, 정책 허들로 한국 배터리사에 기회로 작용한
다. 그러나 ‘26년부터 시작된 FEOC와 관세추가 부담에도 불구, 상반기 미국 ESS 시장의 
76%을 (기타사의 국적을 알 수 없기에 제외하더라도) 중국 셀사가 차지했다. 만일 한국 배
터리 유관회사들이 전망하듯 ‘30년 미국 ESS 시장이 160~200GWh 이상, 240+GWh, 혹
은 240~300GWh가 된다고 해도 3사 전체 Capa의 50%가 채 되지 못한다. EV 수요가 뒷
받침되지 않는다면 미국 ESS만으로는 가동률을 80~90% 이상 올리기 어렵다는 의미다. 특
히 동사는 원통형과 파우치를 생산하고 있으나, 원통형은 북미외 지역 TESLA의 판매에, 
파우치는 올해 출하량이 감소하는 것으로 나타나고 있다. 파우치 고객의 각형 채택 비중 
증가를 감안하면 동사 EV향 배터리 출하량 증가를 확신하기는 어렵다.   
긍정적 기대와 동시에 냉정한 점검이 필요한 시점으로 판단한다.

표2 LG 에너지솔루션 3Q26 실적발표 주요 Q&A 
[Q&A]           
Q1-1. 4Q26 실적 전망과 2027 년 큰 방향은? 
  A. 4Q26 ESS 출하량은 북미 수요 대응으로 QoQ 약 40% 증가 예상 
   유럽 고전압 Mid-Ni 파우치 및 원통형 물량도 연말까지 견조한 출하 지속 전망 
   3Q26 가동을 시작한 북미 JV 효과가 더해져 전사 매출은 QoQ 약 10% 성장 가능할 것으로 예상 
   가동률 개선과 사이트 안정적 램프업은 지속 추진하나 3Q26 일회성 요인 소멸의 기저효과 존재 
   4Q26 혼다 JV ESS CAPA 추가 가동 준비에 따른 초기 고정비 부담으로 수익성 일부 둔화 불가피

2027 년 사업계획은 수립 중이며 전방 수요 변수로 구체적 가이던스 제시는 어려운 단계 
    ESS: 북미 확보 수주 기반으로 유의미한 물량 성장 지속 기대 
    소형: 전략 고객향 EV 원통형과 46 시리즈 신규 애플리케이션 판매로 안정적 성장 예상 
    EV 파우치: 중저가 제품 물량과 북미 EV 향 생산품이 더해져 성장 예상 
   전 사업부 매출 성장 기대하나 핵심 제품 경쟁력·기존 CAPA 효율화·가동률 극대화·원가 개선에 집중 
   2027 년 매출 성장률·영업이익률 등 구체적 수치는 제시 X

Q1-2. 북미 PFE 요건 충족을 위한 LFP 공급망 전략과 중국 관련 주력 양극재 공급사 대응은? 
  A. 미국 생산세액공제 확보를 위해 직접재료비 내 PFE 소재 비중 요건을 충족하는 공급망 구축 필요 
   회사 설명상 PFE 소재 허용 비중은 2026 년 40%에서 2027 년 35% 이하로 강화. Non-PFE 비중과 혼동 유의 
   소재별 소싱 전략을 구체화해 준비 중이며 요건 충족은 차질 없이 진행될 것으로 판단 
   재료비 비중이 큰 양극재는 최근 계약한 국내 업체 외 복수 Non-PFE 업체 조달 옵션 확보

음극재·분리막·전해액도 기존 Non-PFE 공급망에 더해 신규 업체 적극 발굴 
   기존 원가 중심 최적화에서 정책·지정학 리스크를 고려한 공급망 안정성 강화로 전략 확대 
   협력사의 원가·기술·물량 대응력을 종합 평가하고 장기적으로 현지 협력사 발굴·현지화 추진 
   질문한 중국 관련 주력 공급사의 개별 지위·조달 비중에 대한 직접적인 상세 답변 X

Q2-1. ESS 사업의 수익성 전망 및 개선 방안은? 
  A. EV→ESS 라인 전환으로 수요에 맞는 생산체계를 구축하고 북미 고객 적기 공급에 주력 
   다수 사이트 동시 램프업에 어려움이 있었고 고정비 절감 노력에도 수익성 개선 속도는 시장 기대보다 느렸음 
   2026 년 말 50GWh 목표 셀 라인 램프업은 마무리 국면. Link CAPA 도 3Q26 에 이어 4Q26 추가 보강 예정 
   CAPA 안정화와 점진적인 고정비 부담 완화 기대 
   확보 CAPA 효율화, 소재 소싱 다변화로 원재료비·물류비 절감에 집중 
   AI 데이터센터 및 SI 프로젝트 비중 확대를 통한 고객 포트폴리오 최적화 추진 
   셀·시스템 고용량화로 원가 경쟁력을 높여 구조적 수익성 개선 추진 
   구체적 ESS 영업이익률 목표나 개선 폭은 제시 X

Q2-2. 북미 ESS 추가 수주 현황과 향후 모멘텀은? 
  A. 빠른 시장 성장 및 Non-PFE 에 우호적인 정책으로 북미 현지 생산 셀 수요 급증 
   현재 계획된 CAPA 기준으로 2028 년까지 물량은 대부분 확정. 그 이후 물량 중심으로 고객 수주 협의 진행 
   기존 전력망·유틸리티 고객, 이들을 통한 AI 데이터센터 BESS 수요, 하이퍼스케일러 BTM 직접 구매 수요 포함 
   예상 수요가 공급 가능 CAPA 를 초과해 전략적으로 중요한 프로젝트 중심으로 미래 잔여 CAPA 운영 계획 
   추가 고객명·신규 계약량 등은 제시하지 않음. 예상 수요와 확정 수주를 구분

Q3-1. 46 시리즈의 구체적인 수주 현황과 향후 모멘텀은? 
  A. 기존 OEM 부터 EV 스타트업까지 이미 440GWh 이상 물량 확보 
   추가로 다수 글로벌 OEM 과 협의 중이며 가까운 시일 내 의미 있는 규모의 수주 성과 기대 
   제품 경쟁력·지역별 생산 역량으로 46 시리즈 선도 입지를 강화하고 고객 포트폴리오 확대 계획 
   440GWh 는 확보 물량이며 연간 판매량이나 생산 CAPA 가 아님. 추가 계약 규모·고객명은 미공개

### Page 7

Company Report / LG에너지솔루션 / 2026. 11.4 
LS Securities Research 7

Q3-2. Tabless 2170 의 경쟁력과 수주·신규 시장 대응 현황은? 
  A. 기존 제품 대비 낮은 발열·저항으로 안전성 및 고출력에 유리해 HEV·BBU·로봇 등 다양한 용도에 적합 
   오창에서 전동공구용 제품은 이미 양산 중  
   BBU 향 LFP 2170 은 2Q27 양산 목표로 준비 중 
   HEV 향은 2027 년 말 애리조나에서 양산 계획  
   LFP 2170 은 NCM 대비 열안전성 테스트에서 우수한 성능을 보여 고객의 긍정적 피드백 확보 
   고객 니즈·수요에 적극 대응해 신시장 경쟁력 확보 추진. 수주 규모 등 정량 정보는 제시하지 않음

Q4-1. 재무 건전성 개선 방안과 2027 년 자금 조달 계획은? 
  A. 선제 CAPA 투자 자금은 저리 정책자금 장기차입 및 원화·외화 회사채로 안정적으로 조달해 왔음 
   주요 투자는 대부분 집행됐고 CAPEX 는 2025 년을 기점으로 유의미하게 감소 중 
   향후 영업현금흐름 확대분을 차입금 상환에 우선 활용, 자산 유동화를 통한 추가 재원 확보도 병행 
   차입금·금융비용 부담을 축소해 재무지표 개선 및 건전성 확보 추진 
   2026 년 본사 추가 차입 계획은 없으며 해외법인은 기존 약정 차입금 일부 추가 인출 예정 
   2027 년은 기존 차입금 차환·운전자금 범위에서 최소한의 자금 조달 계획 
   영업현금 확대·투자 부담 완화에 따른 잉여 재원으로 차입금 감축 기조 지속. 구체적 조달 규모는 미제시

Q4-2. 유럽 IAA 정책 방향에 대응한 중장기 점유율 확보 전략은? 
  A. IAA 는 아직 최종안 미확정이나 수정 권고안상 외국인 직접투자·현지화 요건 강화 예상 
   배터리뿐 아니라 주요 소재의 현지 생산 역량 중요성이 확대될 전망 
   유럽 OEM 의 공급망 다변화로 현지 생산 체계를 갖춘 비중국 업체 소싱 니즈 확대, 경쟁 환경은 우호적 판단 
   폴란드 현지 양산 및 소재·부품 공급망을 이미 구축해 현지 배터리 소싱 수요에 신속 대응 가능 
   고전압 Mid-Ni 에 대한 유럽 고객 관심 증가. 폴란드 감가상각비 감소도 가격 경쟁력 확보에 기여 
   고전압 Mid-Ni 외 LFP·CTP 등 다양한 제품·솔루션으로 신규 수주를 확보해 유럽 점유율 확대 추진

Document 285: Anthropic承诺约9000亿美元算力开支，债市预期其债券为垃圾级

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic承诺约9000亿美元算力开支，债市预期其债券为垃圾级.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

尽管 Anthropic 上市募资逾千亿美元、市值达 1.18 万亿美元，但因尚未盈利且背负约 9000
亿美元巨额算⼒⽀出承诺，债市普遍预期其将被评为 BB 级 “ 垃圾债 ” 。在科技巨头债市承压
及芯⽚商拟缩减贷款担保的背景下，公司短期仍需依赖结构化融资，并⾯临约三年窗⼝期
以争取投资级评级。
摘要：
Anthropic 上周上市募资逾 1000 亿美元、市值约 1.18 万亿美元，但同期对外承诺未来数年的算⼒
⽀出⾼达约 9000 亿美元。公司尚未盈利、成⽴不⾜六年，债券市场普遍预期评级机构将把其债
券 定 为 投 机 级 （ 垃 圾 级 ）， 多 家 ⼤ 型 债 券 基 ⾦ 经 理 预 计 落 在 BB 区 间 。 这 ⼀ 问 题 不 仅 关 乎
Anthropic ⾃⾝的资本计划，也将牵动芯⽚⼚商的贷款担保安排与整个 AI ⽣态的融资结构。
据《华尔街⽇报》报道，多位债券基⾦经理预计 Anthropic 将获得 BB 区间评级，距投资级仅差
⼀到两档。若如此，它将成为美国市值最⼤的⾼收益债券发⾏⼈，远超⽬前约 800 亿美元市值的

Hilton Worldwide 。
Anthropic ⾸席执⾏官 Dario Amodei 在 CNBC 周四的采访中表⽰： “ 显然，未来某个时点我们希望
达到投资级。 ”Anthropic 发⾔⼈拒绝置评， Fitch Ratings 与 S&P Global Ratings 的发⾔⼈也均拒
绝置评。
评级结果直接影响市场准⼊。投资级评级将为 Anthropic 打开规模 8.6 万亿美元的⾼等级公司债市
场，这对芯⽚制造商是解脱 —— 博通和英伟达已为购买其芯⽚、再租给 Anthropic 及 OpenAI 等 AI
实验室的买家提供了数百亿美元量级的贷款担保，分析师对这类 “ 循环 ” 融资安排和担保规模的担
忧持续升温，芯⽚⼚商希望实验室⾃⾏举债。
债券市场的疑虑
“ 任何 Anthropic 的债务交易都会被⽤怀疑的眼光看待， ” 总部位于旧⾦⼭的 Osterweis Capital
Management 债券基⾦经理 Craig Manchuck 表⽰。“ 你如何给⼀家承诺未来⼏年⽀出 9000 亿美元

的公司定价？ ”
这⼀疑虑的背景是 AI 打破了市场惯例：过去由股权投资者为未经检验的技术初创公司提供资⾦，
只拿利息、不享上涨空间的债券投资者则敬⽽远之。但数字基础设施的建设成本 —— 按某些⼝
径⾼达 10 万亿美元 —— 已超过股市所能提供的规模，迫使⾏业转向举债。
债 券 基 ⾦ 经 理 们 表 ⽰ ， 由 于 ⾼ 收 益 债 市 场 规 模 相 对 较 ⼩ （ 1.7 万 亿 美 元 ） 且 借 贷 成 本 ⾼ ，
Anthropic 不太可能⼤量发⾏⽆担保债券。 PGIM 全球信⽤研究主管 Brian Barnhurst 称，公司更可
能继续通过结构化融资、芯⽚融资等⽅式获取资本， “ 这些⽅式有提升⾃⾝信⽤质量的途径 ” 。
⾏业融资环境趋紧
近⼏年，融资成本上升与信贷市场收紧已成为打压股票市场的⼒量之⼀，数万亿美元市值蒸
发。 Anthropic 上周回吐了 IPO 后的多数涨幅，英伟达、 Meta Platforms 、 Oracle 等⼤市值公司同
样被卷⼊抛售。
债券经理们已在艰难消化⼤型 AI 公司（即 “ 超⼤规模⼚商 ” ）今年发⾏的债券洪流，其中不少表现

不佳。 SpaceX 在 6 ⽉ IPO 后不久发⾏的 250 亿美元债券中，有⼀部分此后跌幅超过 10% 。

Document 322: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53.2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities in the coming 6-12 months, 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15-20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid-to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid-cycle bull market. Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex-UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low-teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities. We expect the 
segment to generate 10-15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years, with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High-Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks, MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advances in oncology and innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks 
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. Healthcare and 
consumer staples accounts for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management in a mid-
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and potentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge 
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two, and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets, private equity provides a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, infrastructure offers differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where contracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3-7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell-off caused by AI-
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi-sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010-2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

The views expressed herein are based on current conditions, subject to change and may differ from other JPMorgan Chase & Co.
affiliates and employees. The views and strategies may not be appropriate for all investors. Investors should speak to their
financial representatives before engaging in any investment product or strategy. This material should not be regarded as
research or as a J.P. Morgan Research Report. Outlooks and past performance are not reliable indicators of future results.

2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK

### Page 4

4
2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
As we approach 2027, we are faced with two competing trends. Capital, as defined by
interest rates, is delivering higher base returns, so our expectations for required returns to
invest have risen. At the same time the need for this capital continues to grow to deliver the
opportunities, and support the requirements, on the horizon. Companies and governments
are looking to investors to fund the opportunities and priorities ahead, at a time when capital

is more expensive and investment decisions must clear a higher bar.
In last year’s Outlook, we explored how three forces, artificial intelligence (AI), global
fragmentation and inflation, were defining a new market frontier. In 2027, that frontier must
be funded.
Companies need capital to realize the full potential of AI. Governments need capital to
strengthen security and resilience in a fragmented world. At the same time, inflation,
economic growth and central bank policy continue to shape the investment environment.

With cash earning a credible return, investors can afford to be discerning about where they
put capital to work.
That is especially true of the AI build-out. Markets are beginning to question whether the
scale of investment will ultimately pay off. We believe AI is a transformative technology
that will fundamentally change how we live and work. We also believe investors need to be
selective, with a focus on the companies that will define this new frontier.

In this environment, portfolio diversification remains critical. Forward-looking returns for
stock-bond portfolios are the most attractive in years, but market volatility creates a wide
range of possible outcomes. A diversified portfolio and a long-term perspective can help
investors navigate both.
The new market frontier is starting to take shape. As investors consider the opportunities
ahead, we are proud to serve as your financial partner and remain committed to helping you

achieve your long-term goals.
Foreword
David Frame
CEO,  J.P. Morgan Global Private Bank

What we got Assessment

Scorecard for the 2026 Outlook

### Page 6

6
2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
In last year’s Outlook, we highlighted their Promise and Pressure,
and since then the salience of these themes has only grown.
AI offered the potential for higher corporate profits and faster
productivity growth, but also the risk of overinvestment,
exuberance and disruption. Fragmentation favored resilience and
security, but at the expense of cost, efficiency and government
budgets. Inflation had retreated from its post-pandemic peak but

seemed structurally higher and more vulnerable to rolling shocks.
Through 2026, markets moved to reflect the world we envisioned,
in some cases more emphatically than we expected.
Demand for AI continued to far outstrip the supply of AI
computing power (“compute” in the tech sector’s shorthand).
This has rewarded the companies designing, producing and
providing the semiconductors, components and power required
to deploy AI at scale. AI model providers generated real revenue

as businesses began paying up for AI-driven productivity gains.
Companies viewed as vulnerable to disruption underperformed.
Conflicts in Iran and Ukraine continued to highlight deep
vulnerabilities in energy supply chains and defense inventories.
The companies that we believe strengthen economic and national
security resilience outperformed even as traditional defense
companies lagged. Inflation remained above central bank targets,
bond yields climbed to multi-decade highs and a global rate-hiking

cycle kicked into gear.
Against that backdrop, a traditional balanced portfolio seems
set to deliver high single-digit returns. Real assets (such as
infrastructure and commodities) and active strategies (such
as diversified hedge funds) have earned their keep as portfolio
diversifiers.
The three forces (AI, fragmentation, inflation) continue to define
the economic and market environment. And they’re interacting
in sometimes unexpected ways that investors need to understand.

One theme links the three: An enormous demand for capital in both
the private and public sectors is raising the cost of capital. (It’s the
law of supply and demand we remember from Economics 101.)
Investors like you set the price for that capital. That is a position
of strength. It also compels decisions that cannot be deferred.
As many investors look to protect—as well as expand upon—several
years of handsome equity market gains, those decisions become

even more important.
Technology companies need your capital—trillions of dollars’
worth—to fund the next phase of the AI build-out. Governments
need it to finance security, resilience and rearmament initiatives.
Consumers, companies and sovereigns all need it to adapt to an
inflationary, fragmented world.
But inflation, resilient economic growth and central bank policy
mean that cash is more attractive. Today, it earns a real return.
Every financing decision, from a hyperscaler’s data center to a

government’s defense budget to your own portfolio allocation,
now carries an opportunity cost that was obscured for most of
the last cycle.
The AI boom will continue only if investors like you are willing
to provide the financing to bridge the gap between infrastructure
spending and consistent revenue. You will share the cost of
security with governments through taxes, inflation or both, but
you can also decide to own the beneficiaries of that investment.

And a higher cost of capital means every one of these decisions
must now clear a higher bar.
The old investing playbook may not power your portfolio through
the current cycle, even if forward looking returns for traditional
stock-bond mixes are still attractive. In the post–global financial
crisis cycle, policymakers kept the cost of capital low to incentivize
risk taking and boost asset prices. Today, capital has a price. We
believe there is a wider range of possible outcomes and, in the

context of public markets that have rarely been this concentrated,
an increased need to consider exposures you may not have.
Capital is the bridge to a world that is more secure and
productive. Your capital sets the price of progress. Place it wisely.

INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL11
In 2027, central banks will matter again. Before you place your capital, you must make a
judgment about how far central bank policy rates will rise. Inflation is key to that decision
for central banks—and for your portfolio.
We believe that the Federal Reserve and other global central banks are raising rates to
adjust their policy stance and influence inflation expectations. They are not looking to

lower demand. If we are right, the expansion can continue, and our constructive view
on multi-asset portfolios will hold. If we are wrong, the cycle could end with a hiking
campaign that breaks economic demand and pressures risk assets.
While U.S. inflation has remained above the Fed’s 2% target, that reflects supply-driven
factors such as energy prices. The labor market is stable and wage growth is decelerating.
Thus, we believe that inflation will begin to moderate. We see no sign of a nascent

wage-price spiral or overheating economy that the Fed would need to correct with an
aggressive rate hiking campaign.
The European Central Bank (ECB) seems to be running a similar playbook, signaling it will
continue hiking only if it sees further effects of higher energy prices. On the other hand,
the People’s Bank of China (PBoC), facing persistent disinflation, seems likely to keep
policy accommodative for the foreseeable future.
Last year, we argued that we are in a new

regime where transformative technologies
and a changing geopolitical environment
drive investment returns more than
traditional forces such as GDP growth
or central bank policies.

### Page 13

13
INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL
13
Inflation containment: Cost of
capital, not the policy rate, is doing
the heavy lifting
In the post–global financial crisis period, unprecedented monetary
policy (zero and negative interest rates and quantitative easing)
coupled with robust forward guidance from the Fed anchored
sovereign rates and compressed credit spreads and equity risk
premiums. In other words, capital had an “artificial” price. Today,

after substantial interest rate hikes, quantitative tightening and
the discontinuation of forward guidance, the price of capital is
more real.
Investors have as much to do with that change as central bankers.
Even before September, when the Fed raised its policy rate 25
basis points (bps), yields were already on the rise. Year to date the
10-year U.S. Treasury has risen roughly 75bps to around 5%. Fifty
of those basis points arrived in September alone, placing the move

in the top decile of monthly increases since 1962. Credit spreads
have widened modestly, investment grade (IG) by around 10bps,
high yield by roughly 40bps.
Importantly, markets are expecting more than two additional
hikes in the current cycle (the next one in December). That means
a higher cost of capital already constrains growth and inflation,
even absent an aggressive Fed.
What are the risks to our view?
What might compel the Fed to kick off an aggressive hiking cycle?

A stronger-than-expected economy, with wages accelerating
ahead of productivity, could force the Fed’s hand. So too could a
sustained move in 5-year/5-year inflation breakevens above 3%.
Indeed, the trajectory of data center construction is important
for bond yields. Ten-year U.S. Treasury yields peaked as investors
began to question further AI capex in 2027. A reacceleration in AI
infrastructure investment could push yields higher.
If higher diesel prices depress economic demand, it would present

a particular challenge to policymakers. In the United States, the
cost of diesel has risen to nearly $6.30/gallon from ~$3.80 before
the Middle East conflict began. Diesel at $10/gallon for a quarter
could make a meaningful dent in economic activity. The Fed would
then have to choose between hiking into a weakening economy
or risking that inflation expectations move higher. This is just one
example of the potential that the next growth downturn could be

accompanied by stubborn inflation, which would pose a challenge
for more traditional portfolios.
INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL

INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL
Yes, inflation is still a threat
to your capital
Inflation is the foundation for our economic outlook, and we
believe it should also be reflected in the foundation of your
investment plan. Even though we expect inflation to moderate
through 2027, it still poses a key risk to the purchasing power
of your capital.
Many of our clients may also need to use a different benchmark
than policymakers and economists. From 2019 through 2025, the

headline PCE index rose at a ~3.5% annual rate. Over the same
period, based on our own clients’ spending data, average ticket
sizes on luxury goods purchases increased at more than a 6%
rate. Maintaining purchasing power for the goods and services
that matter most to you likely requires a higher hurdle than the
headline inflation index would suggest.

### Page 15

15
INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL
The investment
implications
We believe that fixed income markets are
compensating investors appropriately to take on
the risks of sticky inflation and higher policy rates.
Investment grade yields in the United States are
above 6% for the first time since the global financial
crisis. Long-dated municipals yield roughly 5%,
equivalent to an ~8% taxable-equivalent yield.
At the end of the day, bond investing is math, and today’s bond

math is unusually friendly to investors.
At today’s all-in yields, rates would have to rise roughly another 100bps from here
to wipe out a year of IG carry, a worse outcome than even our bear case. Yields may
not have peaked for the cycle. But we believe that investors who add to high grade
credit today can realize solid returns over the medium term even if global central
banks tighten more than we expect and you experience short-term mark to market

losses along the way.

### Page 16

16
Beyond investment grade, preferred equity looks to be well 
positioned to deliver attractive returns to portfolios. In fact, we 
would rather fund U.S. and Canadian banks through preferred 
equity than common equity. Headline yields are around 7% and 
they are a tax efficient asset for U.S.-based investors. Many of the 
catalysts that could drive bank stock outperformance have played 
out. Still, collecting high-single-digit, tax-efficient returns  
by owning bank preferred stock seems attractive from a risk-
reward perspective. 
Private credit may also be a counterintuitive beneficiary of a rising 
rate environment. Consider: In direct lending, realized loan default 
rates range from 1% to 3%, but a broader “shadow” default rate
1

puts loans under stress at ~15% of the business development 
company (BDC) universe.
2
 Investors are compensated for taking 
on this risk: Spreads including discount at issue have widened 
by about 100bps year-to-date. At a ~5% default rate and ~50% 
recovery, loss-adjusted yields on senior-secured direct lending still 
clear ~7.5%.
3

Higher borrowing costs also present opportunity for distressed 
managers who can charge a high price for liquidity, and we are 
seeing new potential in places such as  private credit secondaries 
and asset-backed finance. 
In a world where capital once again has a price, fixed income is 
where that cost is being paid most explicitly to you.

### Page 17

### Page 18

18
We are more positive on core fixed income than we were last year.
Our long-term outlook for traditional multi-asset portfolios that
mix public equities with core fixed income suggests competitive
returns over the next 10-15 years.
However, we see three reasons to add other diversifiers
to portfolios.
First, in large part due to the uncertainty associated with
the potential payback of AI investment, there is a wide range
of possible outcomes.
Options that protect against a sharp decline in equities are trading

at their highest price of the year. Insurance protection against
default for investment grade companies that are borrowing to
finance the AI infrastructure build is approaching levels more
commonly seen in junk issuers. Assets like diversified hedge fund
strategies tend to perform well when volatility is elevated and
could thus help limit portfolio drawdowns. For example, macro
hedge funds have delivered positive performance on average
during quarters when both stocks and bonds are down.

Second, the threat of inflation shocks driven in part by
geopolitical fragmentation should lead to a positive
correlation between stocks and bonds.
That suggests that a traditional 60% stock/40% bond portfolio
is not prepared for a scenario where stocks and bonds are both
falling. Core real assets and commodities respond to inflation
differently than stocks and bonds. Both deserve the consideration
of your long-term capital.
Third, public equity markets have rarely been this

concentrated, largely because of the dominance of AI and
technology more broadly.
The 10 largest stocks in the S&P 500 account for nearly 40%
of the index, the highest share since at least 1980. Of course,
concentration in technology and AI-linked names has driven
strong performance, which we believe will continue.
Still, investors should consider alternative drivers of capital
appreciation. For instance, U.S. core private equity has a 24%
weight to technology and adjacent companies relative to the

S&P 500 at nearly 50%. In exchange for liquidity, private equity
provides the opportunity for capital appreciation without the
concentration of technology. Manager selection is crucial,
though, especially in an environment where software is at risk of
disruption and leverage-driven returns will be harder to generate.
A balanced portfolio
is only the beginning.

force we highlight, is spurring
growing demand for capital from
governments, many of which carry
high levels of debt and deficits.

### Page 21

21
Sovereigns need capital to defend themselves in a new era of
conflict, where $70,000 drones can be competitive with million-
dollar missile systems, defense inventories are depleted, and the
U.S. security umbrella is not what it once was.
4
Governments will
increasingly need capital to finance their AI ecosystems (“compute
sovereignty” may become a new catchphrase). And sovereigns
certainly need capital for energy security. Conflict in Iran and the

closure of the Strait of Hormuz were first a shock and then
a sobering reality, and the ongoing conflict has only exacerbated
the fragility of global energy supply chains, to say nothing of the
long-term threats from climate-related risks.
Where will the missing capital come from? You will be
asked to share the cost through some mix of tariffs, taxes,
inflation and higher rates.
Global fragmentation should also force an assessment of
geographic diversification. Be thoughtful about which currencies

you save in, which sovereigns you lend to, and consider the global
spectrum of potential beneficiaries.
Which sovereign blocs have
earned your capital?
Global fragmentation should force you to think more critically
about capital allocation across sovereign blocs. Traditional metrics
such as trade balances and policy credibility are useful, but you
must also consider a country’s natural resources and its exposure
to critical bottlenecks. Can a country defend itself? That question

has new resonance.
We suggest assessing sovereign risks against their vulnerability
to four criteria.
• Conflict. Can the sovereign provide its own security, or is
it dependent on outside support at prices that may prove
unsustainable?
• Compute. Does the sovereign have its own AI infrastructure
and research ecosystem? Is it prepared to defend against
AI-enabled cyberattacks?
• Chokepoints. Can the sovereign rely on domestically run
supply chains, or does it depend on foreign suppliers?

• Climate. Is the sovereign vulnerable to the negative impacts of
climate change, and is it positioned to invest in adaptation?
We think this framework can help identify which countries
and regions might be best positioned for the era of global
fragmentation. The bottom line is that all the blocs that we
care about as capital allocators have vulnerabilities to mitigate.
Concentration in one is probably not prudent.
4    Center for Strategic and International Studies. Drone Saturation: Russia’s Shahed

Campaign.  2025; CSIS. The Cost and Value in Air and Missile Defense Intercepts. 2025.

### Page 22

22
22
First, the United States seems relatively well-positioned to
weather geopolitical fragmentation over the long term, but
there are still critical risks to address. Fiscal capacity, resource
endowment and more limited bottleneck vulnerability all argue
in favor of Treasuries and the dollar. But there is a risk in central
banks and reserve managers diversifying their savings away
from the United States. U.S. policymakers and business leaders
are beginning to address competitive vulnerabilities in areas

such as semiconductor manufacturing, skilled engineering labor
and rare earths. The ongoing El Niño could cause disruption in
insurance markets and food prices, and we are waiting to see
the potential response from insurers and policymakers.
Europe: Market outlooks diverge among equity,
sovereign credit and corporate credit.
While bondholders are funding capital requirements into needed
resiliency spending, stockholders can benefit from that spending.

Europe’s challenges include elevated oil and gas prices
(most European nations are not energy self-sufficient), rising
government bond yields, high levels of debt and more frequent
and severe heatwaves and drought. In France, 10-year government
bonds now yield more than their Italian counterparts for the first
time since the creation of the eurozone, a roughly 130bps swing
from early 2024. (At the end of Q2, France’s public debt-to-GDP
ratio was 119% versus 140% for Italy.) French elections in 2027,

which will likely  produce a contest between candidates on the far
left and far right, loom large.

Here, we make a few observations
that can guide capital allocation decisions.

### Page 23

23
GLOBAL FRAGMENTATION
European policymakers on the national and pan-European level 
have called for greater investment in defense, technology and 
energy security in lieu of expanded welfare programs. We are 
also monitoring the potential for broader adaptation spending to 
mitigate the adverse impacts of a hotter, less predictable climate. 
German defense spending has doubled since 2022, with Germany 
notably harnessing its industrial infrastructure to diversify away 
from automobile manufacturing.
5
 But the broader push for 
investment has encountered political resistance and limitations  
in scaling up capacity. Only about a third of European Union 
counter-drone and short-range air defense programs are expected 
to be complete by 2030.
6

Given the emerging fiscal strains for European sovereigns, 
we prefer to place our capital in European multinational 
corporations over sovereign debt.
We continue to invest in European equity markets, where 
corporate earnings have proven stronger than expected. Capital 
has flowed to Europe’s beneficiaries of global fragmentation. 
The ranks include the dominant Dutch company that prints 
every advanced chip and French and British defense companies 
capturing rearmament orders.   
Asia: Different blocs, different trades
Geopolitical fragmentation plays differently across Asian markets. 
China is in many ways a beneficiary of the trend. It is an industrial 
and manufacturing power, with an enviable supply of energy. The 
country remains a major exporter (its trade surplus continues 
to set records) and it is building a parallel AI ecosystem with 
sophisticated low-cost models. But economic growth remains 
weak and uneven as its property sector continues to struggle and 
domestic consumption recovers slowly. In September, fixed asset 
investment fell 7.2%.
7

A U.S. or European investor who is considering allocating capital 
to China must also weigh the potential for alignment risk—that is, 
the potential for diverging geopolitical interests with China.  
On balance, we opt for a selective and relatively small allocation 
to Chinese markets, but you shouldn’t ignore it entirely. 
Korea, Taiwan and Japan sell what a fragmented world needs, 
e.g., semiconductors, AI infrastructure and shipbuilding 
equipment. Korea holds roughly 70% of global DRAM capacity, 
while Taiwan dominates advanced chip manufacturing.
8
We remain constructive on Korea and Taiwan equities and 
selectively positive on Japan.  
Latin America: Opportunities for capital in 
nearshoring and commodities
Latin America offers exposure to two related opportunities, the 
nearshoring that is accelerating amid global fragmentation and 
a bull market in commodities.
We see Mexico as a clear beneficiary of nearshoring. The United 
States–Mexico–Canada Agreement (USMCA) remains intact, 
despite political rhetoric from the U.S. administration. Mexico’s 
manufacturing base and deep capital market links to the United 
States underscore the country’s investment potential. That 
potential may increase as firms sell higher value-add exports and 
services into U.S. markets. In fact, tech products now make up 
nearly 50% of Mexico’s exports, and the United States receives 
40% of its imported servers from Mexico.
9
 For investors, it’s 
helpful, too, that the peso is a high-carry convertible currency. 
The rest of the region offers investors a commodity-FX trade: 
Chile and Peru sit on roughly a quarter of the world’s copper 
reserves.
10
 Brazil not only adds iron ore, agriculture and oil, but 
also already hosts 218 data centers, with the potential to double 
capacity by 2030.
11
 As investors in the region, we favor risk assets 
over currencies. We still favor Latin America, even after the Brazil 
election surprise, with a special preference for Mexico and a more 
neutral stance on Chile and Peru.

### Page 24

24
The case for gold and commodities 
In a fragmented world, we believe that gold and other commodities may be  
worth considering as additions to portfolios.
Turning first to gold, many countries have expressed a desire to hold savings in  
something outside the dollar-based system and broadly diversify away from fiat 
currencies.  As a result, central banks have increased their gold holdings as a share  
of overall reserves. The shift hasn’t been uniform, but IMF data shows the dollar’s 
share of official foreign exchange reserves has fallen to roughly 57% from a peak  
of 70% in 2000.
12
Commodities such as copper, oil and gas serve as vital components in compute supply 
chains and national resiliency initiatives. Electrification and grid enhancement help drive 
a projected 25% supply shortfall of copper and 40% shortfall of lithium by 2035.
13

We expect supply shortages to persist even as producers ramp up to meet growing 
demand. New copper mines, for example, take more than 16 years to build.
14
 Importantly, 
too, exposure to commodities offers a portfolio hedge to higher inflation. In fact, over  
the last 50 years commodities have delivered positive returns on average in quarters 
when stock and bond prices both decline.
Despite constrained supply and ongoing structural demand, the stocks of commodity  
and natural resource companies are generally less expensive than the overall market.  
The global energy and materials sectors trade at a 33% and 15% discount to the  
S&P 500, respectively, and sport higher dividend yields.

GLOBAL FRAGMENTATION

### Page 25

25
Do security spending beneficiaries
deserve your capital?
Global fragmentation has supported businesses that benefit from
security spending. The trend will continue, we believe. Indeed,
global fragmentation could be one of the best reasons to
invest globally in equity markets. Most countries face security
vulnerabilities they need to address and most markets host the
companies that benefit from the spending, from Japan and Korea
to France, Sweden and Mexico.

Generally, buyers of security-related products and services
care more about reliability and quick delivery than price. As
we’ve discussed, supply shortages of critical components like
transformers (with lead times between 2.5 and 3 years) and
copper will persist for the foreseeable future.
15
That gives security
companies pricing power and order books that run for years.
Strong earnings (seven of every 10 stocks we follow on this theme
have positive earnings revisions over the last 12 months) and

rising expectations have pushed a basket of security stocks to
a nearly 25% return year to date. We expect continued earnings
growth will drive the next leg of outperformance. Active stock
selection will be critical, though, in separating the market’s
winners and losers and identifying the firms that deserve
your capital.
Where we are placing
our capital
We are placing our own capital behind the need for security
spending in a fragmented world. JPMorganChase’s Security

and Resiliency Initiative (SRI) is a USD 1.5 trillion, 10-year
plan to facilitate, finance and invest in industries critical to
economic security and resiliency.
16
The firm will make direct
equity investments for an initial amount of USD 10 billion in select
companies, primarily in the United States but also in Europe,
to help enhance their growth, spur innovation and accelerate
strategic manufacturing. The remainder of the pledge is designed
to secure debt and loan financing for similar initiatives. Some

recent examples include a financing commitment to MP Materials,
a rare earth metals company, and an equity investment in Shield
AI, a leader in defense technology and autonomous aircraft.
We are also focused on the USD 1.3 trillion upgrade needed for
Europe’s electrical grid.
We are committed to doing our part to address the vulnerabilities
that are inherent in a fragmented world.

2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
Will the AI
capex boom
ultimately

Part 3

### Page 27

27
WILL THE AI CAPEX BOOM ULTIMATELY PAY OFF?27
Investors must consider four related issues:
• How far and how fast will AI adoption (early use of AI models)
and diffusion (more extensive use across the economy) progress?
• How many trillions will AI need to deliver to generate returns
for investors?
• Will markets be willing to finance the infrastructure
build-out, and at what price?
• Which layer of the equity stack will be best positioned to generate returns:

the infrastructure layer that builds the AI data centers,
the compute layer that rents out the semiconductors, or the
application layer that turns model tokens (chunks of text)
into durable business revenue?
The AI build-out has created a staggering
demand for capital. Going forward,
political pressure will increasingly
impact the capex boom.
Since 2022, AI capex has totaled over
USD 2 trillion, with trillions more
expected in the next several years.

Will the capex continue?
Yes, if investors like you fund it, and
regulations allow it. And, perhaps more
importantly for long-term investors:
Will AI spending ultimately pay off?
These may be the most important
questions for investors heading into 2027.

already own U.S. equities, you have been a passive participant.
That’s because, as our Chairman of Market and Investment

represents over half of the S&P 500 market cap, 66% of earnings

17
We believe in AI’s long-term potential to boost productivity and

corporate profits. But investors are asking serious questions
about the magnitude of the infrastructure build, and markets
may be starting to shift their perception of who the ultimate
winners will be.

### Page 29

29
WILL THE AI CAPEX BOOM ULTIMATELY PAY OFF?29
If AI adoption and diffusion stall, nothing else really matters. The infrastructure  
gets stranded, the compute rents compress and the applications never find their  
potential customers. 
Data tells us that these are still very early days for AI adoption. According to Ramp,  
a financial technology company that tracks business spending, 80% of AI spending is 
driven by 1% of businesses, overwhelmingly technology startups buying tokens from 
other technology startups.
18
 In an analysis of AI and productivity, J.P. Morgan Asset 
Management estimates that only 6% of U.S. work hours are AI-assisted today, even  
as two-thirds of tasks may be theoretically exposed.
19

To justify the trillions of capital targeted for the AI build-out, adoption and diffusion must 
broaden beyond the technology sector and deepen beyond pilot programs. We think this 
will happen, for three reasons.
First, enterprise adoption is shifting from experimentation to productivity. Most large 
enterprises remain skittish about AI agents, which operate with minimal human 
involvement. But as corporate leaders grow more comfortable with the technology, 
we think they will increasingly deploy agentic models that will boost productivity and 
compute intensity per user.
Second, the history of transformative technologies tells us that falling prices unlock 
use cases faster than they compress revenue. Since 2023, the price of a given level 
of AI capability has fallen at a 13x annual rate, which is four times faster than DNA 
sequencing.
20
 Over the same period, annual run-rate revenues for the leading model 
providers have grown from zero to well over USD 100 billion.
21
 Goldman Sachs projects 
a 24x increase in token consumption through 2030 as use cases expand.
22
 J.P. Morgan 
Asset Management projects that overall AI adoption could rise to 40% of today’s tasks by 
2040.
23
Third, consumer agents, which can autonomously handle a range of tasks, could 
gain traction. Meta’s Muse has emerged as one contender in this arena. In many 
large economic sectors like healthcare and transportation, AI models still have a low 
penetration rate, and that too could change.
24

The bear case for AI adoption is straightforward: AI remains a productivity tool for 
technologists and a novelty for everyone else. The error rate remains too high and 
enterprise adoption stalls while most consumers decide to take a pass on the new 
AI spreads through  
the economy—or  
all bets are off

### Page 31

31
WILL THE AI CAPEX BOOM ULTIMATELY PAY OFF?31
AI spending only pays off if the economy generates enough 
revenue from it, and does so quickly enough, to cover the cost of 
the capital deployed (especially data centers). Given the sheer size 
of the current pipeline, and anxiety about how much of it moves 
from plans to completed projects, we expect growing investor 
pressure to see that revenues do in fact materialize.
To understand the challenge, consider what it takes to break even 
or pay for itself year to year, including the cost of investor capital. 
A gigawatt of AI data center capacity costs roughly USD 40 billion. 
To cover its cost of capital, a 1GW facility must generate about 
USD 11 billion in revenue per year.
26
  The ~129GW of global frontier 
data center capacity expected by 2029 would therefore need to 
generate about USD 1.5 trillion a year in compute revenue, largely 
sourced from the application companies that must rent computing 
power to produce products and services that are consumed by 
enterprises, consumers and governments. These companies, 
in particular the private model labs, likely need to earn 50% 
margins above their computing costs. That means end-customer 
AI spending would need to total roughly twice that amount, about 
USD 3 trillion per year. 
So how much revenue could the AI ecosystem earn in the early 
2030s? Under reasonable assumptions, we estimate:
• Enterprises could spend about USD 2.5 trillion a year on AI 
through employee AI tools and agents.
• Consumer sectors could add about USD 750 billion in revenue 
through advertising and subscriptions.
• Governments could add another USD 400 billion.
27

That gets AI revenue to about USD ~3.5 trillion, about USD 700 
billion above the breakeven rate needed for the data centers to 
pay for themselves year to year. That surplus represents returns 
for investors in the compute and application layer. It may seem 
sizable, but it is based on an addressable market that is merely an 
estimate and far from assured.
A closely watched risk is whether a large share of AI-driven 
revenue, especially from large enterprises, comes from job 
displacement—replacing workers, rather than creating new 
How many trillions  
does AI need to deliver? 
demand. If half of potential enterprise AI spending replaces roles 
that would otherwise be filled, and AI costs about half as much 
as the worker it replaces, AI would absorb roughly 24 million 
knowledge worker jobs worldwide over five years, or about 1.3% 
of the global knowledge workforce each year. That is disruptive, 
but it is about a third of the pace at which U.S. manufacturing shed 
its workforce in the 2000s (~3.4% a year) amid factory automation 
and China’s entry into the World Trade Organization. Much of the 
AI adjustment could come through roles left unfilled rather than 
layoffs. The bigger risk is concentration: Displacement that falls 
heavily on specific occupations or regions could test political limits 
well before the aggregate numbers do.
28

To illustrate, if two-thirds of those displaced roles were in the 
United States, the U.S. would absorb roughly 16 million workers 
over five years, or about 3 million a year. That is roughly 2% of 
payrolls annually, more than four times the pace of the 2000s 
manufacturing decline. It would approach the rate of job loss 
during the Great Recession, only sustained for five years rather 
than two. Even if most of AI job displacement avoids layoffs, it 
would at the very least eliminate  the economy’s normal annual 
job growth. As the midterm election results suggest, it seems very 
unlikely that there would be political tolerance of this outcome. 
J.P. Morgan Asset Management’s analysis of AI’s impact on the 
economy suggests that job disruption is more likely to occur within 
jobs rather than eliminate jobs outright. After all, every job is 
ultimately a collection of tasks, and to date, AI can automate or 
augment only a portion of them. Moreover, while we anticipate AI 
diffusion across the economy will be profound, it will take time, 
and adoption is expected to be uneven and gradual. In our view,

this steady uptake should help limit the risk of widespread
job displacement.
Turning back to the economics of the AI build-out: The gap
between the revenue required for the AI ecosystem to break even

and the potential revenue from sources such as enterprises and
consumers is a critical variable. When the potential market is far
larger than what the AI ecosystem needs to break even, every new
data center looks like a solid investment. But as the infrastructure
pipeline grows toward the size of the addressable AI market, the
gap shrinks, and so do the incentives to keep building.
We believe the infrastructure pipeline is entering a more
selective phase. First, the addressable market is still very much

debated, especially after frontier model lab revenues decelerated
recently. Second, bond markets have started to balk at excessive
leverage and aggressive infrastructure plans from smaller AI
players. Finally, overbuilding is often how capital investment
booms end. The railroads and the internet were both tremendously
productive innovations. But far too much track and fiber were laid
before demand arrived. As a result, capital markets suffered severe

losses. In the AI cycle, though, more restrictions around data center
development reduce the risk that capacity exceeds demand.
Going forward, companies will find it difficult to convince
investors that they need additional data center capacity beyond
the current pipeline especially in light of the likelihood of a
more onerous regulatory environment. Therefore, value may
increasingly accrue to companies that control the crucial
bottlenecks, or have the pricing power to work through constraints

such as political pressure, regulatory shifts, power availability, grid
access, permitting speed and financing.

### Page 33

33
WILL THE AI CAPEX BOOM ULTIMATELY PAY OFF?33
As even casual observers of the AI story understand, financing will be key to
how the plot unfolds. As a result, before you underwrite the equity at any layer
of the AI ecosystem, you should be comfortable with the debt on a company’s
balance sheet. (Another lesson from Economics 101: In a debt default, equity
value disappears.)
Some AI companies have recently hit the limits of what public debt markets

will tolerate. Consider the example of unsecured bonds from CoreWeave,
a “neocloud” (typically highly leveraged providers that rent out their compute
capacity). Their bonds trade at distressed level spreads well over 1,000bps.
Does this signal the beginning of the end of the AI boom?  No, but it does
suggest a different opportunity set within public and private markets.
Although AI debt is growing (AI-related issuance will top USD 300 billion this

year), debt growth as a share of GDP is still much lower than previous historical
leverage cycles and more political hurdles to data center development could
also result in a slower pace of leverage growth.
